The quota for the time limits already laid down remains to be confirmed. At the end of the examination of the draft Constitutional Reform Act, the competition decree will be discussed.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u16	DIVINE (LN-Aut). Mr President, let us say that we want to close a game by force, giving satisfaction to those who, at a date that is completely devoid of political significance, wanted to attribute a sign of political virility. Opening the sitting at 9.30 a.m. today, announcing that, without interruption, we will have to make explanations of vote at 9.30 a.m. tomorrow morning, working 24 hours a day exhausted the minimal times we had available, means starting a procedure that is certainly unusual for the Italian Parliament, but also for the world parliaments that, moreover, does not have equals even in the countries that approached democracies yesterday the other. Defining aberrant the coming to build a new Constitution by these methods is still remaining in euphemisms. We know that the Constitutions born in the various countries are distinguished according to the needs and how they were born, and are divided into long or short Constitutions, rigid or flexible, ottriate or voted. The doctrinal panorama of the classifications of the Constitutions still lacked a term: the Fake Constitutions. We will give this opportunity to the doctrine to insert a new category, the Farce Constitutions, realized with tests of force, without dialectic and without the help of oppositions, disinteresting in the varied composition of a country that needs to recognize itself in a single fundamental Charter, that recognizes both the rights of majorities and oppositions. A country that does not recognize the rights of political minorities in building the fundamental rule, is a country that must be ashamed, whose political class is not worthy of this name. We really do not know what reactions this choice will cause, because if we had little time until yesterday to incorporate our suggestions for change today we are told that we can no longer intervene on those because, after the times, we do not talk but we vote day and night. We do not know what kind of reaction this will cause in our Group, and probably in oppositions. We do not know what kind of attitude we will have to take, because following this path, this track you have taken, perhaps as a majority you will be able to bring home this provision but you will certainly crash. [[Applause from the LN-Aut Group.]]
ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u17	I'm asking to talk.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u18	You have the right to do so.

In the sense that it is curious that they remain out of this emergency, from this urgency that makes us propose our work to all things without continuity, the fundamental questions that we were recalled by the Parent Company of the 5 Star Movement, to which I add those prepared by the same Government, I think of the decree competitiveness. Therefore, we are absolutely willing to ensure that all the fundamental questions that can give answers are honoured with our work, these yes immediate, even if partial, to the country, that today wakes up with information that does not reassure and that do not leave serene - who can - for a well-deserved period of rest. So, concluding, if it is proposed to go all the way, whatever it is, but at all round and on the issues that really, not only passionate, but concern Italian citizens. ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u22	SCILIPOTI (FI-PdL XVII). Mr President, I asked for the floor. Unlike what someone thinks I have the greatest respect for, this is not about protagonism, but only about making reflections. Without prejudice to the fact that I will vote as indicated by my group, I would like to make a small thought, however, to say that physiologically, because of my modest medical skills, I do not think that we are in a position to do a good and profitable job by running our work without interruption. In my opinion it would have been more appropriate (even if someone wonders what I am saying and thinks that I do not realize what I am saying) to go ahead as previously set out in the timetable with the interruptions, to give everyone in this House the opportunity to be serene and to vote with much serenity, continuing Monday, Tuesday or Wednesday. There is no urgency to stop the reform immediately. We are all willing to vote for this blessed reform; none of us intends to hinder the path, but I think it is also legitimate to think about doing things with serenity and tranquility in the interests of everyone, of the country. So, to conclude this reflection of mine, and to address in a particular way to someone whom I esteem, it is not a matter of protagonism, but of just saying what we think. If anyone wants to eliminate the possibility of expressing what we think, we might as well not come to Parliament anymore, we do a delegation, we only vote for the Parent Company and good night. Without prejudice to the fact that I am in line with what the party will say in this House, allow me to reflect aloud and to make the consideration that normally and physiologically it would not be appropriate to continue work in excess of a physiological problem and because the organism will not be in a position to do that work that should be carried out with serenity and in the interest of the community and the country.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u228	FINDING, rapporteur. Mr Azzollini, I am sorry, I understand very well the meaning of your speech, but let me say that the first paragraph of Article 97 contains a provision of principle, namely that public administrations, in line with the European Union's legal order, ensure balance of budgets and the sustainability of public debt. What we are discussing here is the matter of the budget, because we are thinking about legislative competence. To sum up the issue, thanks to the suggestions received, both from the amendments of Senator Lanzillotta and Senator Quagliariello, and from the important amendments of Senator Azzollini, we would have achieved with our amendments the following arrangement: the reinforced legislative procedure, precisely described in the fifth paragraph of Article 70, is applied, which we are examining, for the laws of stability and budget, with a reinforced power of interference in the legislative procedure of the Senate, when it comes to matters concerning the Regions, which are those specifically listed in the fourth paragraph of Article 70. This is the result of a limitation that already existed in the text, but that the Lanzillotta and Quagliariello amendments have made explicit. By then collecting the appropriate suggestions from Senator Azzollini, we add to the fourth paragraph of article 70, and then insert in the enhanced procedure provided for there, also the law of order. Adding the first paragraph of Article 97, President Azzollini, seems superfluous because it is not a matter but a principle affirmed by the Constitution. It seems to me that we have satisfactorily covered the whole matter, with reference to the subjects referred to in the fourth paragraph of Article 70, when, in the first paragraph of Article 81, we have provided that the enhanced competence of the Senate is limited to that area.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u237	FERRARA Mario (GAL). Madam President, I would like to address the scholars present in this House on constitutional problems and developments in the world, to clarify something that we have spoken to President Azzollini on several occasions over the years and which I would like to draw attention especially to Senator Quagliariello, a great scholar. There are two systems of democracy: the federal one and the non-federal one. The greatest example in the world of federal democracy is the American one, in which the budget has equal reading: the House or rather the Congress and the Senate face it equally. In France there are no regions and, therefore, the problem does not arise and the Senate has no competence in this matter. In Germany, with a special ruling by the German Constitutional Court, it was decided in 1984 that the Bundesrat had no legislative power, which is why there is no need for the budget to be read equally in the two branches of Parliament, because there is no second branch of Parliament. But if the Senate, for the statements that have been made constantly in this House, will continue to be a legislative body, it is absurd and impossible that in a pseudo, para or otherwise federal system such as the Italian one, where there are the Regions, the budget is not equal reading. This will mean an absolute decompensation of the system of verification of expenditure and - as President Azzollini rightly says - will have grounds for fundamental unconstitutionalism.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u264	Madam President, I seem to recall that there were two agendas, the G10.1 and the G10.236, both accepted. With regard to the opinion, Article 11 is one of the few articles that qualifies this measure in particular, because it addresses the issue of participation and direct democracy, which has always been discussed but never been resolved. Both in the Commission and in the proposals that have come to this House, there has been considerable interest and contribution from colleagues on this issue. In particular, the problem of the legislative initiative entrusted to the proposals of popular initiative law, which, to date, were identified as "acts of faith" has been addressed: no proposal of popular initiative law has ever been approved throughout republican history. In this sense, first the Commission and then the amendment proposed by the rapporteurs intended to increase the number of signatures necessary for the presentation of these draft laws, which the Commission proposed to 250 000, which may have been an excessive number, which the rapporteur's proposal in this House reduces to 150 000 signatures, receiving a number of amendments which went in this direction. This increase, however, means that Parliament's regulations must provide for certain deadlines for discussion and final decision on the proposals. There has also been an analysis of the possibility of providing for an examination time defined in the Constitution but, at the present time, we have considered that this tightened up the system too much and could have led to a blocking of the legislative function of the Chambers. However, in this sense we have gone even further and, taking my particular cue from the proposals made by Mr Lo Moro in the Commission, which developed the destiny of the proposals for popular initiative also with the anticipation of a referendum not defined in the text, but which I would like to define as confirmation or deliberative, the orientation of the rapporteurs has been to take this path and to follow it. This, however, is a major step forward in the direction and direction of the referendums, which I believe to be a major step forward in relation to the current state of our Constitution and an extension of very strong direct participation. As a result, the rapporteurs have tabled amendment 11,5000, which incorporates in particular Senator Lo Moro's amendment 11.66 (text 3) as well as a part of Senator Palermo's amendment, but I must say that the contribution in this regard has come from all the members of the Commission. There is, therefore, an invitation to withdraw from all the other amendments and this is a sincere invitation, because I believe that most of the requests have been received by the rapporteurs. On the other hand, on the agenda, I am opposed to the G11.1 agenda, while I am in favour of the G11.94 agenda at the first signing of Senator Bignami, perhaps with a slight rewording. Madam President, I would have needed more time to explain why we have tabled sub-amendments, both on this point concerning the laws of popular initiative and on the subject of the referendum. Of course, we have seen a few steps forward from the first text but, of course, there is still one fundamental issue. We would have needed, within this constitutional reform, a much more significant and total strengthening of the instruments of direct democracy for all the reasons we have tried to explain in these days and, in particular, to try to put an element of counter-trend in relation to the crisis of politics and the crisis of representation. Our sub-amendments are more expansive and try to make these instruments of direct democracy more practicable and more traversable. We think that 150,000 signatures are too many and that 100,000 would suffice. We think that the referral back to the proposed referendum, which is proposed to a constitutional law, makes it more difficult to implement. Instead, it would have been much more sensible to set a deadline and automatically transform the law of popular initiative into a referendum after collecting a subsequent number of signatures. More inclusive instruments were needed, which could have been instruments more capable of overcoming the crisis element of representativeness. The original text was totally unsatisfactory to us. This is a slightly better text than the original one, but we continue to think that on this issue it would take more courage and, indeed, a more effective approach.

By voting in favour of Amendment No 11,5000 by the rapporteurs, I find a point that does not work from a technical point of view. When we say 'as well as other forms of consultation', it is as if direct democracy and participatory democracy were the same thing. That is not the case, and I say it at least for Parliament's work, so that in the future it is clear to everyone that we are talking about radically different phenomena. Direct democracy, in fact, is a tool that is based on majority logic; the participatory one, instead, mitigates the majority logic by anticipating the decision as soon as possible through wider involvement. This is the theme of the future. All the... [[The microphone automatically switches off]] Madam President, we have time.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u314	Madam President, I would like to give the House an account of the effects that the general debate has had on the attitude of the rapporteurs. The first Commission dealt with the issue of the referendum with a view to finding a more advanced balance between the needs, forms and instruments of representative democracy and those of direct democracy. As my fellow Members will recall, the text before the House, i.e. the text approved in the Commission, brought profound innovation. Let me say a few words precisely because I would like to prevent a superficial consideration of the work done by the Commission from leading to hasty judgements, of course excluding what is the legitimate political exploitation of the judgments that are expressed in this House. With the text approved by the Commission, we introduced two substantial changes. The first was that the quorum, on which to assess the validity of the referendum, was led to the majority of voters in the last parliamentary elections. Of course, if colleagues reflect on what was the attendance at the last consultations, they will realize that this was a rule that certainly facilitated the possibility of the referendum being valid. The second novelty concerned the possibility that, in the middle of the collection of signatures set at 800,000, the Constitutional Court could intervene to judge the admissibility of the referendum. This, undoubtedly, was for those who collected signatures, in the event that the Court had ruled on the admissibility of the referendum, a powerful engine for continuing the collection of signatures and thus, easily, reaching the goal of the further 400,000 signatures. Many Members have contested the fact that the number of signatures needed between before and after had been quantified at 800,000. I do not want to refer here to the need to update the number of signatures needed for the referendum in relation to the increase in the population from 1948 to the present, which, as things stand, would lead to the number of 800,000. The rapporteurs, however, understood in the course of the debate that the overall system - certainly never applied and for which, therefore, it was not possible to assess the convenience of those who were keen to use the instrument of the referendum - had strongly criticized the version voted by the Commission. That text, on the other hand, I repeat that it had every intention of really being a step forward in that balance between the needs of representative democracy and the demands of direct democracy, which had such a broad echo not only in this House but also in the country. In view of the debate which began and which identified, in the original number of 500,000 of the signatories to the referendum, the number to be considered appropriate, the rapporteurs presented a new text, 15,5000 (text 2), which colleagues will be able to find on page 42 of the annex, in which two hypotheses are envisaged. The first hypothesis is that the referendum retains the need for a collection of 500 000 signatures or a request supported by 5 regional councils, but it also provides, in case the proposal subject to referendum has the support of 800,000 voters, that the quorum is not calculated on the number of those entitled to vote, but on the majority of voters in the last elections of the House of Deputies. This text skips that scrutiny of the Constitutional Court we were talking about earlier. Let me say that the fact remains that the work which the rapporteurs have considered to be submitted to this House has tried to be as reproductive as possible of the majority of the consensus in this House, accepting many points, some of which probably also have a strong symbolic value - I am referring to the number of 500 000 - but certainly correspond to assessments made by the individual political forces about the use they intend to make of the referendum instrument. Having tabled this text leads to an opinion against all the amendments and an invitation to withdraw them. There is also the agenda for G15.1 on which we are only prepared to give a favourable opinion if it is reworded. I will take care to get the Presidency reworded.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u315	Okay. I call on the Government representative to comment on the amendments under consideration.

ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u320	De Cristofaro (Misto-SEL). Madam President, the speech is always the same and is very similar to that made for the laws of popular initiative. Here too, we understand the rapporteur's proposal and realize that a step forward is being taken in relation to the first text. However, we reiterate that at this time, precisely because Part II of the Constitution is being rewritten, a real effort had to be made, at least on this, to "change direction," as the Government has told us many times over the last few weeks. We would have liked a much more substantial strengthening of the institutions of direct democracy, especially in view of the current political crisis. At first, there had even been a worsening. Now, at the most, there is a maintenance and certainly not that improvement that would have served and that could be assured in a very simple way: to predict the mobile quorum and relativize it to the quorum of the old parliamentary elections and to fix to 650,000 the number of signatures, raising it to the 500,000 currently planned and reducing it to the 800,000 foreseen by the first draft. This wording would have ensured a much simpler mechanism, while we fear that the system of mobile quorums and, above all, of double quorums may cause, over the coming months and years, a concrete difficulty for all the sponsoring committees which will seek - precisely - to use the instruments of direct democracy.

Furthermore, Mr President, I understand your loneliness, because when I practice my profession I am always very lonely. If I'm wrong, it's manslaughter or serious personal injury, and in those forty seconds of time I have to give an answer, in those moments of great difficulty and loneliness, I always remember when as children you used to play foosball and if the ball stopped in the corner it was said: "ball contends, ball defense". ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u730	GIROTTO (M5S). In the passage between the Senate and the Chamber and - now - the return to the Senate has happened something quite important: indeed, extremely important. The International Monetary Fund (i.e. not a censorable body) has issued its latest report on the calculation of the correct pricing of fossil fuels and has completely changed its attitude, in the sense that, at last, it has defined that taxation must re-understand externalities: the taxation of fossil fuels, i.e., must be raised considerably to re-understand, at last, externalities. A battle that several groups of civil society have been fighting for several years, has now also received the legitimacy of the International Monetary Fund. Why do I say that? As you all know, this decree-law also contains rules relating to electricity and has done exactly the opposite: instead of going to find resources from fossil sources that are directly and indirectly subsidized, it has gone to touch what is the remedy, that is, renewable sources. The subsidies to fossil fuels have not been touched at all; the big discounts, the big incentives that energy companies have, have not been touched at all. I remember that the energy companies are a very small minority of the Italian production fabric, because, as you all know, 95 of Italian companies are made up of small and medium-sized enterprises, which - therefore - have not very important consumption and find themselves paying a higher bill. It was therefore certainly right to reduce the cost of the electric bill for small and medium-sized enterprises, but it was touched exactly what was not to be touched, namely renewables, and instead fossils were not touched, which had to be touched and - on the contrary - are not at all encouraged to be efficient. I am referring to the energy companies, which have discounts that are calculated above a certain level of consumption; these companies therefore have no interest in going below that level of consumption because - otherwise - they would lose those discounts. This is therefore an absolutely energy-suicide policy. On the contrary, with the famous rule on the spread-incentive and on the burdens of self-consumption, one of the few supply chains that, since 2008, was in contrast with the great financial crisis. I remind everyone that the renewables chain - it is the official data of ENEA - has lost 70,000 jobs in 2012 and we still don't have the data for 2013, but surely the trend is always towards a loss of jobs. So what was a supply chain that could have given us great satisfaction in terms of employment is in serious danger. This measure - let us remember - is yet another that goes to clear the margins of profitability of the renewables supply chain. That is why we are strongly opposed to this measure, because it is going to depress one of the few counter-trend supply chains which will have huge growth margins throughout the Mediterranean basin in the coming years. PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. Please conclude, Senator Girotto. GIROTTO (M5S). In this case, the so-called competitiveness decree-law does not deserve the title with which it is entitled. Well, the case of condo-hotel served precisely to allocate some of those beds, through the introduction of a different real estate case, which could give some oxygen to these companies. The amendment concerning the use of cash for foreign tourists could have meant a lot to our tourism companies, in terms of attractiveness to the foreign market, especially to Eastern European countries, which can give great numbers and represent a great potential for our hotel companies. Well, both the amendments, with a blow of acceptance, were cut and thus eliminated what had been a very positive contribution not only of the Forza Italia Group, but more generally of all the work done in the 10th and 13th Commissions in the Senate. This gives us a little bit of the amount of trouble in this competition decree. We could continue with other examples in brief: a series of micro-measures, small tips and small grants involving companies involved in the energy, agriculture and industry sectors. I would like to point out one thing about all of them: the decree, which already deals in itself with a series of matters in a disorganised way, also posed the problem and the objective of achieving greater simplification, especially for the fulfilment of agricultural undertakings. There is little or no trace of this, and I do not think that we can talk about the competitiveness of our agricultural sector and our agricultural enterprises thanks to the rules introduced by the decree today when we examine it. Let me give another small example, because sometimes you can have an opinion even looking at just a few aspects of the decree, without power in five minutes to deal with all the issues addressed by the decree: a rule on the environment. Many have emphasized the possibility of giving breath back to an agricultural tradition that was that of the burning of the branches, of the waste prunings in the individual soils. Well, this may seem romantically a reference to what was an agriculture and an Antan economy, but it conflicts with environmental protection interests of some importance. We cannot tell entrepreneurs to build biomass plants and then take away part of the product from them. This type of economy also contrasts with a very important interest, which is that of the right to health, enshrined in Article 32 of the Constitution. In fact, we are going to burn the pruning waste knowing that dioxins are released into the atmosphere which are particularly insidious and harmful to human health and those who are engaged in the work of the fields. We could give many other examples. I'll just quote one more. The unevenness of the decree is such that it allows to introduce a norm ad personam that allows to save the career post Region of the commissioner delegate for the emergency earthquake in the person of Vasco Errani. Paragraph 4 provides for the only remaining Chamber, probably made up of nominations (because members will not be chosen with preferences, as there will be blocked lists), of people who owe the party the chair and, therefore, not to their own political path, to their own electorate who has chosen and elected them. Therefore, these are not the offices of a strong electoral mandate that makes them free, because the electoral mandate, strong of choice, makes free from a party that can change scene and dress during the work of government. I enter with a party and - then - the party makes a pact with my enemy, the one with which it said, throughout the election campaign, that it would never make a pact. But I cannot give up because I was chosen by that party, by that party leader and I know that if I say a voice against it, I will no longer have that function. This can be, on the one hand, an act of cowardice (in the sense that I want to remain attached to my armchair), on the other, I might also think that my function in that party and in politics is just and functional, so I don't want to leave it because I think I can make an improvement path. But in the meantime, I'm silent until I have a secret vow. Let's go back to paragraph 8 of Article 12. What do you expect for that one House? It provides that the Government may request the Chamber of Deputies to decide that a bill, indicated as essential for the implementation of the government programme, be placed with priority on the agenda and put to the final vote within 60 days of the request, or within a shorter period determined according to the Rules of Procedure, taking into account the complexity of the matter. Then, you identify in this what we have suffered with the decrees-laws. But we no longer call them decrees-laws because, in this provision, these will be well regulated and much better defined, so that they can no longer be used arbitrarily. Instead, we will have these legislative measures aimed at the implementation of the government programme. And what is it? It's not even the election program, it's the government program. Renzi's programme is not the PD programme last February, Renzi's programme, under the Nazarene Pact, is not what the electorate sent him to Parliament for. So what is this government program? It's a will. ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u767	ZELLER (Aut (SVP, UV, PATT, UPT)-PSI-MAIE). Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, this measure has substantial positive aspects for the recovery of the economy. The Senate, at first reading, improved the text of the Decree-Law, including simplification measures affecting the agriculture sector, firstly, with regard to the system of controls, the authorisation legislation for the wine sector and the reduction of obligations for farmers. The reduction and rationalization of controls for agricultural enterprises, for example, through the institutionalisation of the single register, are very satisfactory measures. We insisted, during the examination of the measure, that further simplification and reduction of burdens and bureaucratic constraints should be implemented and strengthened and that some results have been achieved. What we believe is fundamental, today and in the future, is that these measures are organic and strategic. In the parliamentary debate, our proposed amendments, accepted, as well as those of other parliamentary groups, have significantly improved the measure. No less important for small and medium-sized enterprises is to provide a tax credit, for the years 2014-2016, for investments that have as their objective the development of new products. The establishment of the quality agricultural work network and the development of young farmers contribute to a framework of interventions which we consider to be positive. The section on freight tariffs has also been improved, avoiding unsustainable increases in rail transport, which should be facilitated and not penalised. As a Group for Autonomies, in the meeting that the President of the Council had with the majority leaders in the Senate last Friday, we indicated how they should be considered as fully priority on the government agenda, beyond constitutional reforms and electoral law, also interventions that can support the competitiveness of the economic system. The Government, from now on, will have to focus more on solving the real problems of the country, such as the revival of the economy and employment. The measures provided for in this decree can be considered appropriate, are the premise, but certainly not the completion of an economic and industrial growth policy to be implemented in a sustainable time. We hope, in particular, to launch an incisive reform of the labour market on the model of the reforms approved by the Schríder Government and which re-launched Germany ten years ago. I therefore welcome what the President-in-Office of the Council said publicly, when he reiterated the urgency of institutional reforms, but stressed the need to address the real problems and therefore the labour market. What we consider to be negative and, in our opinion, a serious mistake is that the Government, when examining the provision in the Chamber, abolished the rule introduced here in the Senate and on which, as a Group for the Autonomous Communities, we fought together with other political forces, which established that the purchase of goods and services in the tourism and hotel sector was abolished, although with all the necessary measures, the obligation of 1,000 euros for the use of cash for non-resident European citizens. This rule was a balanced and effective mediation which took into account all relevant profiles. It should be clear that the introduction or maintenance of such a constraint is equivalent to subtracting market shares, especially in the tourism sector. The confirmation of the constraint of the 1.000 euros brings our tourism companies back to a strongly penalising condition, which has no terms of comparison with the other European countries and which is creating strong imbalances especially for the border areas. The objective of reducing the energy costs of small and medium-sized enterprises is certainly acceptable, but we believe that the method by which this reduction is financed is highly questionable. Retroactively affecting the facilities granted for the photovoltaic sector is a serious blow to legal certainty and therefore also to the credibility of Italy, in particular by foreign investors. Among the measures of the measure, instead, we welcome very much the rule introduced for the Stelvio Park that constitutes the basis for a new governance and a more effective and shared administration, with the involvement of the territorial authorities concerned. I would like to take this opportunity to thank Vice-Ministers De Vincenti and Olivero for their great sensitivity and excellent cooperation during the conversion of this decree-law. Announcement that, since the overall assessment of the measure remains positive, the Senators of the Group for Autonomies will vote for the confidence placed by the Government. Waiting for cuts, by 15 January 2015, there will be changes in tax rates and reductions in existing discounts and deductions to ensure higher revenues of 3 billion in 2015, 7 billion in 2016 and 10 billion in 2017. The €10 billion to cover the IRPEF bonus of €80 for 2015 is still to be found. The problem is therefore not whether to make the corrective manoeuvre or not: of course the public accounts will have to be corrected in the law of stability for 2015, but that is the test for a clear reversal of trend in Italian economic policy. In the document of economy and finance the Executive had previewed 4,5 billion spending review for 2014, 17 billion for 2015 and 32 billion for 2016. On the contrary, the trend in public spending shows us that it has risen by 7.8 percent compared to the previous year and that it will reach the record figure of 825 billion euros at the end of 2014. It is not conceivable to issue new public debt securities, as the Government did in April for 40 million euros, for the simple moral consideration that we cannot leave to our children a public debt that has now reached 2,164 billion. It was certainly necessary to leave something in the pocket of the Italians, but through a fiscal shock that affected everyone, citizens and businesses. A cut in the rates would have affected all citizens, not only the lucky winners of the Renzi lottery: for businesses it would have been an injection of capital to move investment. That bonus awarded to almost 11 million Italians is unfair because it has been billed to all the others (pensionees, VAT matches and, among them, many young people who, in order to work, bill their jobs, single-income families). With a cut in rates, we would have achieved the additional objective of attracting new taxpayers to pay their taxes, who are now fleeing because they believe our tax system is unfair. We can no longer postpone a drastic cut in unproductive spending: if Italy turns out to be the eighth economic power of the world, although the international rankings of doing business place us in sixty-fifth place, it means that our entrepreneurs, in spite of the many bureaucratic, credit and fiscal obstacles, have a great desire to do business, invent, produce and make known their products and their creativity, exporting them all over the world: it is our famous Made in Italy, that we should try to protect much more. Businesses face improper costs, those of bureaucracy, which represent about four points of GDP: this is the first wedge to be reduced. Each euro invested in businesses and removed from the costs of bureaucratic practices would have a much higher wealth multiplier. It is an elementary observation: we invest in production, we generate wealth and we create work. We will never tire of remembering this: employment creates it by companies and not by the State. Give business and citizens oxygen and the economic recovery will come, of course. For these reasons, I strongly announce the contrary vote of the Forza Italia Group to the confidence on this decree, which is certainly not worthy of Italy and Italians. [[Applause FI-PdL XVII. Congratulations.]]
ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u779	I ask you to speak for an explanation of vote.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u780	You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u781	Mr Tomaselli (PD). Mr President, it has been a very long day and so I will not use the whole ten minutes for the explanation of vote on behalf of the Democratic Group. I will use only a few moments to announce our Group's strong support for this measure. It is certainly a changed measure compared to the first reading in the Senate; in some respects - probably - it has lost some positive elements that had been introduced; in other respects it has earned others that go to strengthen the provision. Briefly, I would like to express a concept: the difficulty that our country is experiencing does not change if the 0.1 of a few trimesters becomes a minus 0.2. Of course, we would all like to see the sign more in the face of the growth rates of our GDP, but this confirms that our country is experiencing a very long recession and a great difficulty. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, representatives of the Government, I believe that we all need to be aware of the serious difficulties that our country is experiencing. This measure is certainly not a solution to these problems, but it is a useful measure to relaunch the competitiveness of the Italian system, made of Made in Italy, small and medium-sized enterprises and agri-food; fact of the need to reduce the energy bill borne by small and medium-sized enterprises. Surely, this measure goes in the direction of the reforms that the Government has initiated and that the majority that supports it is carrying out with conviction. The uncritical exaltation of a single measure will never come from us, but I find propaganda useless, in this context so difficult for our country, to deny everything that this Government and Parliament have put on the field in a few months. From interventions at work, to tax delegation, to forthcoming interventions on infrastructure, to the issue of institutional and constitutional reforms that we have talked a long time about and which we have been interested in in these last few weeks. The issue of constitutional reform, in all probability, does not solve any of the problems that Italian families, Italian workers and companies in our country have. But, if I may say, it gives the authority to Italian politics to begin to strike, on the one hand, the annuities - the too many annuities that this country can no longer afford - and, on the other hand, to have more courage to intervene with the deep reforms that need the economy, the productive system and the system of interests in our country, in the perspective that moves us and along which also this decree-law gives its contribution to relaunch the Italy system and make it more competitive. This is a measure that goes hand in hand with others. I am thinking of the controversy of these days, which is quite unusual, about the 80 euro bonus. Probably few are those 80 euros in the pockets of Italian families at the end of the month, but it is 11 billion euros, I repeat: 11 billion euros. A huge figure that has been decided to move from other incomes to Italian families and workers, to the incomes of employees. It seems to me to be a huge operation of redistribution of wealth and social equity which this Government has taken on and which we have convincedly replaced. For these reasons, we will fully support this measure that will do good to the economy and businesses of our country. In this sense, we will vote in favour of trust as a Group of the Democratic Party. [[Applause from the PD Group]]
ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u782	We shall therefore proceed to the vote.
ParlaMint-IT_2014-08-07-LEG17-Senato-sed-302.ana.u783	I refer to the vote on the single article of the bill n. 1541-B of conversion into law, with amendments, of the decree-law 24 June 2014, n. 91, in the text approved by the Chamber of Deputies, on the approval of which the Government has posed the question of trust. I recall that, pursuant to Rule 94, second paragraph, of the Constitution and pursuant to Rule 161 (1) of the Rules of Procedure, the vote on the matter of trust will take place by roll-call vote. Each senator called by the Senator Secretary will have to vote before the Presidency. The senators for trust will answer yes; the opposing senators will answer no; the senators who intend to abstain will speak accordingly. I now draw by lot the name of the senator from whom the roll call will begin. (The name of Senator Sollo is drawn by lot.) I invite the Senator Secretary to proceed with the appeal, starting with Senator Sollo. SAGGESE, secretary, makes the appeal.
